# Pilot studies (S35): tables

PILOT figures: 50 seeds (1-50), engine 70d23be, not headline figures. Generated by `python3 dev/tools/pilot_studies.py` from BetterToBest/compassionism-sim-results `pilot-*-70d23be`. Every interval is a 95% paired-bootstrap interval over seeds. Effects are the row minus the no-programme row of the same seed; for the share outcomes a reduction is a positive number of percentage points. Every estimate in these tables is also in `estimates.csv`.

## 1. Timings (for S47)

Core-seconds per seed and per row, summed over every process of every shard (each process runs every block on its own seeds), from the shard logs. Runners: GitHub-hosted, 4 vCPU, 15 GB; 2 or 4 processes per runner. "Rows" is the number of rows in the block. The full-batch estimate is 500 seeds times the per-seed figure.

Runner-hours: the summed wall time of the pilot's shards times 10 (50 to 500 seeds), with the same processes per runner. A 4-vCPU runner gives about two cores' worth of work: with 4 processes per runner (C, D, D10, F) each process runs about half as fast as with 2 (compare the per-row figures of C with A, D with B), so core-seconds overstate the work there; runner-hours are the figure to plan with.

## 3. Population size (Q1): study A, 500 to 10,000 adults

Delta = the figure at N adults minus the figure at 500 adults; each size resampled on its own (independent runs). Scale rule: |Delta| above the half-width of its interval and above 1 point (5% of the 500-adult value for cost). "Passes" marks a scale effect under the rule.

## 6. Participation (Q4): study D at 500 adults, D10 at 10,000

Effect of the release row at each starting participation level; departure = effect at q minus q/90 times the effect at 90%. Realised participation in year 20 (`jnP19`) beside it. Non-linear: departure interval excludes zero and at least 1 point. S5's detection level: the lowest level where all three of outcomes 1-3 (design-neutral BLEI, cost of living, wealth at year 20) have intervals excluding zero in the favourable direction.
